2. **Llama-4-Scout-17B-16E-Instruct download**: In progress (PID 27565), downloading via ModelScope to `/root/autodl-tmp/models/Llama4Scout`. Log shows 50 weight shards, each ~4GB = ~200GB total. Download speed ~1MB/s per shard, 8 shards downloading in parallel. At ~1MB/s average, each 4GB shard takes ~1 hour. 

With 50 shards and 8 concurrent downloads, that's roughly 7 rounds of an hour each, so about 7 hours total for Llama-4-Scout. But wait—that's 205GB, which is way larger than the 110GB estimate in memory.md. The Qwen3-235B-A22B with 235B parameters in MoE architecture would be even bigger, potentially ~470GB in FP16 or compressed with INT4 quantization.

This creates a serious disk space problem. I've got 350GB total, currently using 89GB, so even the optimistic scenario of 89 + 200 + 120 = 409GB exceeds capacity. If Qwen3-235B ends up being FP16 at ~470GB, it's completely infeasible. The Llama-4-Scout model with its 16 experts might have more total parameters than the 17B base suggests, so I need to figure out the actual size before committing to this approach. ~4GB = 200GB in safetensors (FP16/BF16). The memory.md says INT8 would be ~115GB. But the download is FP16 weights (safetensors), not quantized. Quantization would happen at load time with bitsandbytes.

So the raw weights are ~200GB for Llama4Scout and likely ~400-500GB for Qwen3-235B (FP16). Both won't fit on the 350GB disk even individually for Qwen3-235B.

Actually, wait. Qwen3-235B-A22B is a MoE model too. "A22B" means "Active 22B" — so 235B total params, 22B active per token. In FP16, that's 235B × 2 bytes = 470GB. In INT4 (GPTQ/AWQ), ~120GB. But ModelScope `snapshot_download` downloads the full model as-is, likely FP16 safetensors.

This is a critical disk issue. Both models at FP16 = 200 + 470 = 670GB. Way over 350GB. The Qwen model ID `"Qwen/Qwen3-235B-A22B"` on ModelScope is almost certainly the full FP16 version, not a quantized variant which would have a different name. I need to flag this immediately as a blocker. So neither model will fit on disk together with the existing data. I should post a critical warning about this storage constraint right now.

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nEvaluate cross-lingual transfer of English correctness probes.\\n\\nTwo transfer conditions:\\n  (a) Zero-shot: apply English probe directly to target-language activations\\n  (b) Mean-shift: shift target activations by (mean_en - mean_tgt) before probing\\n\\nInput:  English probes (.pkl) + per-language activations (.pt)\\nOutput: transfer_results.csv with AUROC / ECE / Brier per (layer, language, method)\\n\\nUsage:\\n  python transfer_eval.py \\\\\\n    --activations_dir ./activations \\\\\\n    --probes_dir ./probes \\\\\\n    --output_dir ./results \\\\\\n    --layers 0,4,8,12,16,20,24,28,31 \\\\\\n    --languages zh ar hi sw ko\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport os\\nimport pickle\\n\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport torch\\nfrom sklearn.metrics import roc_auc_score, brier_score_loss\\n\\n\\ndef parse_args():\\n    p = argparse.ArgumentParser()\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--activations_dir\\\", default=\\\"./activations\\\")\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--probes_dir\\\", default=\\\"./probes\\\")\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--output_dir\\\", default=\\\"./results\\\")\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--layers\\\", default=None,\\n                   help=\\\"Comma-separated layer indices. Default: auto-detect from activations_dir\\\")\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--languages\\\", nargs=\\\"+\\\", default=[\\\"zh\\\", \\\"ar\\\", \\\"hi\\\", \\\"sw\\\", \\\"ko\\\"])\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--n_perm\\\", type=int, default=1000,\\n                   help=\\\"Number of permutations for significance test\\\")\\n    return p.parse_args()\\n\\n\\ndef compute_ece(probs, labels, n_bins=10):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Histogram-based ECE.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    bin_edges = np.linspace(0, 1, n_bins + 1)\\n    ece = 0.0\\n    for lo, hi in zip(bin_edges[:-1], bin_edges[1:]):\\n        mask = (probs > lo) & (probs <= hi)\\n        if lo == 0.0:\\n            mask = (probs >= lo) & (probs <= hi)\\n        n_in_bin = mask.sum()\\n        if n_in_bin == 0:\\n            continue\\n        ece += (n_in_bin / len(probs)) * abs(labels[mask].mean() - probs[mask].mean())\\n    return ece\\n\\n\\ndef permutation_test_auroc(y_true, y_score, n_perm=1000, seed=42):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"One-sided permutation test: is AUROC significantly above chance?\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)\\n    real_auroc = roc_auc_score(y_true, y_score)\\n    count_ge = 0\\n    for _ in range(n_perm):\\n        perm_auroc = roc_auc_score(rng.permutation(y_true), y_score)\\n        if perm_auroc >= real_auroc:\\n            count_ge += 1\\n    return (count_ge + 1) / (n_perm + 1)\\n\\n\\ndef evaluate_probe(probe, scaler, X, y, n_perm=1000):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Apply probe and compute metrics. Returns (auroc, ece, brier, p_value, probs).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    X_s = scaler.transform(X)\\n    probs = probe.predict_proba(X_s)[:, 1]\\n    logits = probe.decision_function(X_s)\\n\\n    if len(np.unique(y)) < 2:\\n        return None, None, None, None, probs\\n\\n    auroc = roc_auc_score(y, logits)\\n    ece = compute_ece(probs, y)\\n    brier = brier_score_loss(y, probs)\\n    p_value = permutation_test_auroc(y, logits, n_perm=n_perm)\\n    return auroc, ece, brier, p_value, probs\\n\\n\\ndef main():\\n    args = parse_args()\\n    if args.layers is None:\\n        import glob\\n        available = sorted(set(\\n            int(os.path.basename(f).split('_')[0].replace('layer', ''))\\n            for f in glob.glob(os.path.join(args.activations_dir, 'layer*_en.pt'))\\n        ))\\n        if not available:\\n            raise RuntimeError(f\\\"No layer*_en.pt files found in {args.activations_dir}\\\")\\n        print(f\\\"Auto-detected layers: {available}\\\")\\n        layer_indices = available\\n    else:\\n        layer_indices = sorted(int(x) for x in args.layers.split(\\\",\\\"))\\n    os.makedirs(args.output_dir, exist_ok=True)\\n\\n    results = []\\n\\n    def _fmt(v):\\n        return f\\\"{v:.4f}\\\" if v is not None else \\\"  N/A \\\"\\n\\n    print(f\\\"{'Layer':>5s} {'Lang':>4s} {'Method':>10s} {'AUROC':>7s} {'ECE':>7s} {'Brier':>7s} {'p':>7s} {'N':>6s}\\\")\\n    print(\\\"-\\\" * 60)\\n\\n    for li in layer_indices:\\n        probe_path = os.path.join(args.probes_dir, f\\\"probe_layer{li}.pkl\\\")\\n        if not os.path.exists(probe_path):\\n            print(f\\\"  [SKIP] No probe for layer {li}\\\")\\n            continue\\n\\n        with open(probe_path, \\\"rb\\\") as f:\\n            saved = pickle.load(f)\\n        probe = saved[\\\"model\\\"]\\n        scaler = saved[\\\"scaler\\\"]\\n\\n        en_pt = os.path.join(args.activations_dir, f\\\"layer{li}_en.pt\\\")\\n        if not os.path.exists(en_pt):\\n            print(f\\\"  [SKIP] No English activations for layer {li}\\\")\\n            continue\\n        en_data = torch.load(en_pt, weights_only=True)\\n        en_mean = en_data[\\\"activations\\\"].numpy().mean(axis=0)\\n\\n        for lang in args.languages:\\n            tgt_pt = os.path.join(args.activations_dir, f\\\"layer{li}_{lang}.pt\\\")\\n            if not os.path.exists(tgt_pt):\\n                print(f\\\"  [SKIP] No activations for layer {li}, lang {lang}\\\")\\n                continue\\n\\n            tgt_data = torch.load(tgt_pt, weights_only=True)\\n            X_tgt = tgt_data[\\\"activations\\\"].numpy()\\n            y_tgt = tgt_data[\\\"labels\\\"].numpy()\\n            n_samples = len(y_tgt)\\n\\n            # (a) Zero-shot\\n            auroc, ece, brier, pval, _ = evaluate_probe(probe, scaler, X_tgt, y_tgt, n_perm=args.n_perm)\\n            row = {\\\"layer\\\": li, \\\"language\\\": lang, \\\"method\\\": \\\"zero_shot\\\",\\n                   \\\"auroc\\\": auroc, \\\"ece\\\": ece, \\\"brier\\\": brier, \\\"p_value\\\": pval, \\\"n\\\": n_samples}\\n            results.append(row)\\n            print(f\\\"{li:>5d} {lang:>4s} {'zero_shot':>10s} {_fmt(auroc):>7s} {_fmt(ece):>7s} {_fmt(brier):>7s} {_fmt(pval):>7s} {n_samples:>6d}\\\")\\n\\n            # (b) Mean-shift: X_shifted = X_tgt - (mean_tgt - mean_en)\\n            tgt_mean = X_tgt.mean(axis=0)\\n            X_shifted = X_tgt - (tgt_mean - en_mean)\\n            auroc, ece, brier, pval, _ = evaluate_probe(probe, scaler, X_shifted, y_tgt, n_perm=args.n_perm)\\n            row = {\\\"layer\\\": li, \\\"language\\\": lang, \\\"method\\\": \\\"mean_shift\\\",\\n                   \\\"auroc\\\": auroc, \\\"ece\\\": ece, \\\"brier\\\": brier, \\\"p_value\\\": pval, \\\"n\\\": n_samples}\\n            results.append(row)\\n            print(f\\\"{li:>5d} {lang:>4s} {'mean_shift':>10s} {_fmt(auroc):>7s} {_fmt(ece):>7s} {_fmt(brier):>7s} {_fmt(pval):>7s} {n_samples:>6d}\\\")\\n\\n    csv_path = os.path.join(args.output_dir, \\\"transfer_results.csv\\\")\\n    with open(csv_path, \\\"w\\\") as f:\\n        f.write(\\\"layer,language,method,auroc,ece,brier,p_value,n\\\\n\\\")\\n        for r in results:\\n            vals = []\\n            for k in [\\\"auroc\\\", \\\"ece\\\", \\\"brier\\\", f.write(f\\\"{r['layer']},{r['language']},{r['method']},{','.join(vals)},{r['n']}\\\\n\\\")\\n\\n    print(f\\\"\\\\nResults saved to {csv_path}\\\")\\n\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n = permutation_test_auroc(y, logits, n_perm=n_perm)\\n    return auroc, ece, brier, p_value, probs\\n\\n\\ndef main():\\n    args = parse_args()\\n    if args.layers is None:\\n        import glob\\n <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Both PIDs are gone (ps returned exit code 1 with only header). The disk is back to 88GB (was 94GB earlier when 3.3GB of Llama4Scout was present). So both downloads were killed or crashed. The Llama4Scout directory was cleaned up (back to 88GB from 94GB), and the Qwen3-235B never created its directory.

This is very important — the downloads were TERMINATED. The worker is reported as "done" but neither model was actually downloaded. The disk space went back to baseline.

This might be because:
1. Someone (Director/Agent) killed the downloads after my disk space warning


2. The downloads failed on their own
3. The processes were killed when the worker session ended

Killing those FP16 downloads was actually the right call since they wouldn't fit anyway, but I need to check if this was intentional and whether there's a plan to download quantized versions instead.
```

</details>

Now I have the full picture:

1. **FP16 downloads killed** ✓ — old PIDs 27403 and 27565 are gone
2. **Quantized downloads started**:
   - **Qwen3-235B-A22B-GPTQ-Int4** (PID 28071): via ModelScope, downloading to `/root/autodl-tmp/models/Qwen3-235B-A22B-GPTQ-Int4`. Running for ~5 min.
   - **Llama-4-Scout-17B-16E-Instruct-quantized.w4a16** (PID 28924): via HuggingFace mirror (`hf download RedHatAI/...`), downloading to `/root/autodl-tmp/models/Llama4-Scout-W4A16`. Running for ~3 min.


3. **Disk space**: 93GB used with 258GB available, so plenty of room for both downloads. The environment is properly configured with the HF mirror endpoint, SSL certificates, and XET disabled. I'm estimating the final model sizes to see if everything will fit.

4. **Model size calculations**: The Qwen3-235B in GPTQ-Int4 format should be around 120GB, and the Llama-4-Scout with W4A16 quantization would be roughly 50GB based on the original 200GB FP16 size. That's 170GB total, which with the current 93GB used comes to 263GB—leaving about 87GB of headroom on the 350GB total, so it's tight but workable. Let me recalculate the Qwen model more precisely since GPTQ might have different overhead than a simple 0.5 bytes per parameter estimate.

5. **Revised estimates**: The Llama-4-Scout is a MoE model at 17B with 16 experts, so the full FP16 footprint is around 205GB across 50 shards. Quantized to W4A16, that drops to roughly 51GB. Combined with Qwen, I'm looking at 170-180GB of new downloads, which puts total usage at 263-273GB out of 350GB—leaving 77-87GB for experiment data and activations. It's still manageable but leaves little margin for error.

6. **One concern**: The Llama download uses HuggingFace's CLI through a mirror, while Qwen uses ModelScope, and the documentation warns against sourcing the network turbo configuration for these operations.

Actually, looking more carefully at both commands, they both do source network_turbo, but the SSL certificate paths are explicitly set, which should mitigate the proxy cert issues the documentation mentions. The Llama download also overrides the endpoint to use the HF mirror directly, and Qwen's ModelScope has its own CDN, so both should work fine.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Let me now check the experiment plan for the updated P0 details and paper_framing.md for any changes.

Key observations from the ROADMAP and decisions:

1. **D008 (new)**: Clarified mvp_003 is 2/4 PASS, not 3/4. P1 evaluated at best-AUROC layer where ECE fails. This is the correction I flagged. Good.

2. **D009 (new)**: Re-dream processed with same scores (4/3/3). P0 conditionally approved. 6 confirmed actions, 7 observations tracked. Code blockers must be resolved first.


3. **Serial model strategy**: The ROADMAP outlines a sequential approach—Llama4 FP16 first (~200GB), then experiment and delete before moving to Qwen3 GPTQ-Int4 (~65GB). But there's a discrepancy: the current downloads show W4A16 quantized versions instead, and the processes were killed and replaced, so I need to clarify whether the strategy shifted or if the documentation wasn't updated.

Looking at the math: Llama4 FP16 at 200GB would fit across the 4×96GB GPUs (about 50GB per GPU with device_map="auto"), but Qwen3-235B in FP16 would need ~470GB—way over the 384GB total VRAM available, so quantization becomes necessary for that one.

The ROADMAP estimates Qwen3 GPTQ-Int4 at ~65GB, which seems optimistic for 235B parameters (the math suggests closer to 117.5GB for weights alone), but I'll work with what's documented and flag the discrepancy.

The main blockers are clear: the transfer_eval fix, extract_activations_large, and model downloads. The serial loading strategy makes sense given the disk constraints, and the D008 correction addresses the earlier issue I flagged.

D009 remains conditionally approved with unchanged scores—the remaining gaps are implementation-level rather than conceptual. The dream action items are concrete and tied to the experiment timeline. GPU-wise, 189 hours total across 4 GPUs means roughly 47 hours wall clock if fully parallel, which is feasible for the August 3 EACL deadline.

The real concern is that transfer_eval still hasn't been deployed after three failed attempts—this pattern needs direct intervention. And extract_activations_large is still missing, which is critical for the P0 experiments on larger models.

Now I'm noticing some inconsistencies in the resource estimates: the Qwen3 GPTQ-Int4 size at 65GB seems too low for 235B parameters—it should be closer to 120GB. And there's a discrepancy with Llama4 where the strategy now says FP16 at 200GB, but I'm not sure if the quantized downloads that were started are being abandoned or if this is just an outdated roadmap entry. Let me check what's actually in progress.
```

```text
Now I have the complete picture. Let me review the ROADMAP update systematically.

Key observations:

**Positive:**
1. D008 corrects the P1 criteria issue I flagged — both models are consistently 2/4 PASS
2. D009 re-dream P0 approval is well-reasoned — remaining issues are implementation-level
3. Serial model strategy is sound for disk constraints
4. Dream action items (A1-A6) are concrete and well-integrated into experiment plans
5. plan_011 notes now include ALL my CKA concerns (depth confound, per-language limitations, between vs within language distinction)


6. plan_003 now has detailed CaCLR implementation spec
7. plan_004 has grid justification (D12) addressing my earlier concern

**Concerns:**

There's a critical mismatch between the stated serial download strategy and what's actually happening — the ROADMAP mentions Llama4 FP16 (~200GB), but the current downloads are quantized versions (Qwen3-GPTQ-Int4 and Llama4-Scout-W4A16) running simultaneously rather than serially. Additionally, plan_002 specifies Llama4 Scout INT8 but the actual download appears to be W4A16, which is a discrepancy I need to clarify.

The Qwen3 estimates also seem off — the ROADMAP claims GPTQ-Int4 at ~65GB, but 235B parameters at INT4 should require roughly 120GB for full weight storage, though the Agent might be calculating only the active MoE parameters (22B out of 235B) rather than total model size.

Both downloads are happening in parallel right now, which contradicts the serial strategy and risks consuming 170-240GB of new data on top of the existing 88GB, potentially maxing out the 350GB disk. The P0 blockers remain unresolved — transfer_eval.py hasn't been deployed to main yet and extract_activations_large.py is still missing. On the GPU side, 155 hours across 4 GPUs with serial model processing could stretch to 3-5 days of wall-clock time, which is still within the Aug 3 deadline but tight.

Now I'm checking whether both quantized models can actually fit on disk simultaneously. The Qwen3 model at 235B parameters with INT4 quantization should be around 120-130GB, while the Llama4-Scout W4A16 variant, being a quantized version of the 200GB FP16 model, would likely be significantly smaller.

With 88GB already used, downloading both would total around 258GB out of 350GB available—tight but workable if my estimates are accurate. However, there's a discrepancy: the roadmap describes a serial strategy (Llama4 FP16 first, then delete, then Qwen3), but the actual downloads that were initiated are quantized versions running in parallel. The timing suggests the roadmap was written after the download decision was made, so I should note this inconsistency.

ROADMAP 与实际执行不一致\n\n- [severity: warning] **模型下载策略文档滞后**：\n  - ROADMAP 写 \"Llama4 FP16 先（~200GB）→ 串行 → Qwen3 GPTQ-Int4（~65GB）\"\n  - 实际：两个量化版本**同时下载**——Qwen3-GPTQ-Int4 (PID 28071) + Llama4-Scout-W4A16 (PID 28924)\n  - 两处不一致：(a) Llama4 是 W4A16 非 FP16；(b) 并行非串行\n  - 如果两模型量化版同时放得下（估算 88+120+50≈258GB/350GB），并行策略更好。但 ROADMAP 应同步更新\n\n- [severity: warning] **plan_002 写 \"INT8\" 但下载的是 W4A16 (INT4)**。如果最终用 W4A16 而非 INT8，实验条件需更新，且 plan_010（量化验证 ablation）也需同步调整。\n\n- [severity: warning] **Qwen3 GPTQ-Int4 磁盘估计偏低**：ROADMAP 写 ~65GB，但 235B params × 0.5 bytes ≈ 120GB。这影响磁盘预算计算——如果实际是 120GB 而非 65GB，两模型同时存储更紧张（88+120+50=258 vs 88+65+50=203）。\n\n### 4. P0 Blockers 状态\n\n- [severity: warning] **3 个 P0 blocker 尚未清除**：\n  1. `transfer_eval.py` fix：修复版在 `code/transfer_eval.py`，需 cp 到主路径。**已标记 3 次**。\n  2. `extract_activations_large.py`：服务器和本地均未找到。大模型 activation 提取需要 register_forward_hook + INT4 加载 + device_map，现有脚本不支持。\n  3. 模型下载进行中（需数小时）\n\n  Blocker 1 是 5 秒工作量，Blocker 2 需要写新代码。建议 Agent 优先处理。\n\n### 5. GPU 预算与时间线\n\n- [severity: info] P0: 155 GPU-hrs / 4 GPUs = ~39h 理论最短墙钟时间。加上串行模型切换 + 下载等待，实际 ~4-5 天。距 EACL 2027 ARR deadline (Aug 3) 还有 74 天，充裕。\n- [severity: info] P1: 34 GPU-hrs，可与 P0 部分并行（CPU-bound 实验如 plan_011/012/013 不占 GPU）。"
}
```

Hooks capture only the target layers.\\n  - Supports INT4/INT8 quantization via bitsandbytes (--quantization int4/int8).\\n  - Incremental saving via --save_interval to survive OOM or interruption.\\n  - Architecture-adaptive layer access (model.model.layers / model.transformer.h).\\n\\nOutput format is identical to extract_activations.py:\\n  layer{i}_{lang}.pt  ->  {\\\"activations\\\": Tensor(N, Qwen/Qwen3-235B-A22B \\\\\\n    --languages en zh ar hi sw ko \\\\\\n    --layers 0,8,16,24,32,48,64,80,93 \\\\\\n    --output_dir ./activations \\\\\\n    --quantization int4 \\\\\\n    --save_interval 200\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport os\\n\\nimport torch\\nfrom datasets import load_dataset\\nfrom transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer\\nfrom tqdm p.add_argument(\\\"--layers\\\", default=\\\"0,4,8,12,16,20,24,28,31\\\",\\n                   help=\\\"Comma-separated transformer layer indices (0-indexed)\\\")\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--output_dir\\\", default=\\\"./activations\\\")\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--n_shot\\\", type=int, default=5)\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--max_samples\\\", type=int, default=0, help=\\\"0 = all test samples\\\")\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--dtype\\\", default=\\\"bfloat16\\\", choices=[\\\"float16\\\", \\\"bfloat16\\\"])\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--subjects\\\", nargs=\\\"*\\\", default=None,\\n                   help=\\\"Restrict to these MMLU subjects (default: all)\\\")\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--quantization\\\", default=\\\"none\\\", 1. Primary Request and Intent:
   I am operating as an independent scientific reviewer for the `xling_calib_direction` project. My role is to independently audit experiment conclusions, verify data integrity, check experimental design, flag issues via `reviewer_post_to_director`, and review worker outputs when notified. The project investigates cross-lingual calibration transfer via language-agnostic confidence directions in LLMs, targeting EACL 2027 (ARR deadline August 3, 2026). I must always respond in Chinese and follow the reviewer audit checklist. This session focused on reviewing a rapid sequence of worker completions covering: CKA analysis, bug fixes, disk management, model downloads, code deployment, and ROADMAP updates.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Cross-lingual correctness direction transfer**: English-trained linear probes on LLM hidden layers predict correctness in non-English languages
   - **Depth-transferability Pareto frontier**: Architecture-dependent tradeoff — strong on Qwen (4/5 langs), weak on Llama (1-2/5 langs)
   - **Sigmoid saturation AUROC artifact**: At Llama L31, logit mean=+39 → 55% samples predict_proba=1.0 → tied ranks → mean-shift changes ranking. Fix: use `decision_function` (logits) for AUROC, keep `predict_proba` for ECE/Brier
   - **Mean-shift rank-invariance proof**: Under StandardScaler + LogisticRegression, mean-shift adds constant to logits → AUROC invariant (proven, confirmed empirically except for sigmoid saturation artifact)
   - **CKA (Centered Kernel Alignment)**: Cross-lingual representation similarity. Decreases with depth on both models. Between-language correlation valid (p=0.007), within-language mostly not significant (n=9 too small). Depth confound in pooled analysis.
   - **Temperature scaling data leakage**: Original optimized T on test set (T=6.21, ECE=0.074). Fixed with 3-way split: T=0.62, ECE=0.2216 — temp scaling WORSENS ECE. P1 drops to FAIL.
   - **CaCLR**: Proposed method L_BCE + α·L_SB-ECE + β·L_consistency (MMD). Conditional framing: positive if consistency helps, negative result if inert
   - **GPTQ-Int4 / W4A16 quantization**: For fitting large models (Qwen3-235B, Llama4-Scout) on 4× RTX 6000 PRO 96GB + 350GB disk
   - **register_forward_hook**: Required for large models (94+ layers) instead of output_hidden_states=True which caches all layers and OOMs
   - **BitsAndBytesConfig**: For INT4/INT8 loading of quantized models

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`/root/xling_calib_direction/transfer_eval.py`** (remote, NOW FIXED)
     - Critical file for evaluating cross-lingual transfer. Had sigmoid saturation bug using predict_proba for AUROC.
     - Fix deployed (4th attempt successful): Modify time 05:40, size 6461 bytes
     - Key fix in evaluate_probe function (line 73):
     ```python
     def evaluate_probe(probe, scaler, X, y, n_perm=1000):
         X_s = scaler.transform(X)
         probs = probe.predict_proba(X_s)[:, 1]
         logits = probe.decision_function(X_s)       # NEW: logits for ranking
         if len(np.unique(y)) < 2:
             return None, None, None, None, probs
         auroc = roc_auc_score(y, logits)             # FIXED: was roc_auc_score(y, probs)
         ece = compute_ece(probs, y)                  # probs for calibration
         brier = brier_score_loss(y, probs)
         p_value = permutation_test_auroc(y, logits, n_perm=n_perm)  # FIXED
         return auroc, ece, brier, p_value, probs
     ```

   - **`/root/xling_calib_direction/extract_activations_large.py`** (remote, NEW)
     - 12460 bytes, created at 05:42. Required for P0 experiments on large models.
     - Key features: register_forward_hook (line 178), device_map="auto" (line 117), BitsAndBytesConfig for INT4/INT8 (lines 118-128), incremental saving via save_interval, architecture-adaptive layer access, resume from existing progress
     - Functions: parse_args, load_mmlu, load_mmlu_dev, normalize_row, _format_question, build_prompt, load_model, get_layer_modules, register_activation_hooks, remove_hooks, save_layer_data, load_existing_progress, main
     - Output format identical to extract_activations.py: `layer{i}_{lang}.pt → {"activations": Tensor(N, hidden_dim), "labels": Tensor(N,)}`

   - **`/root/xling_calib_direction/code/cka_vs_degradation.py`** (remote)
     - Analysis script computing CKA vs AUROC degradation correlation per model
     - Uses Spearman correlation, both overall and per-language
     - Defines degradation = (en_auroc - tgt_auroc) / en_auroc
     - Cross-model comparison of language rankings by CKA and degradation

   - **`/root/xling_calib_direction/results_mvp003/transfer_results.csv`** (remote, UPDATED)
     - L31 data fixed: all languages now show zero_shot AUROC ≡ mean_shift AUROC (sigmoid artifact resolved)
     - zh@L31: 0.594275→0.615614 (zero_shot corrected upward), now equals mean_shift

   - **`/root/xling_calib_direction/results/cka_results.csv`** (Qwen CKA, 5 langs × 8 layers)
     - L0-L16 high plateau, L16-L27 steep drop. L27: zh=0.344, ar=0.356, hi=0.116, sw=0.084, ko=0.318

   - **`/root/xling_calib_direction/results_mvp003/cka_results.csv`** (Llama CKA, 4 langs × 9 layers)
     - More gradual decline: zh L4=0.930 → L31=0.469. Llama4 Scout has ko missing.

   - **Registry (registry.yaml)**: 3 experiments all DONE
     - mvp_001: Qwen2.5-7B, 2/4 PASS (P2+P3), EN best AUROC=0.7541@L27
     - mvp_002: Qwen complementary layers, 2/4 PASS, EN best AUROC=0.7481@L26
     - mvp_003: Llama-3.1-8B, 2/4 PASS (P2+P3), EN best AUROC=0.7284@L31, includes cka_vs_degradation metrics

   - **decisions.yaml**: D001-D009
     - D008: Clarified mvp_003 is 2/4 PASS (not 3/4). P1 at best-AUROC layer where ECE fails.
     - D009: Re-dream (4/3/3), P0 conditionally approved. 6 action items: CaCLR spec, CKA partial correlation, arch claim qualified, control task elevated, references added, CKA framed as geometric/correlational.

   - **memory.md**: Updated with sigmoid saturation finding, CKA-degradation correlations, large model VRAM estimates, disk info, code fix records

   - **ROADMAP.md**: Updated to reflect parallel quantized download strategy, correct Qwen3 size (~120GB not ~65GB), P0 blockers listed

   - **experiment_plan.yaml**: 16 experiments. P0: plan_001-004 (155 GPU-hrs). P1: plan_005-013 (~34 GPU-hrs). plan_011 notes extensively updated with CKA analysis concerns.

   - **Disk/Server State**:
     - `/root/xling_calib_direction` → symlink → `/root/autodl-tmp/xling_calib_direction` (29GB)
     - Root partition: 4% used (30GB free)
     - Data disk: ~93GB used / 350GB total (~258GB free)
     - Backup at `/root/autodl-tmp/xling_backup/` (29GB)
     - Downloads in progress: Qwen3-GPTQ-Int4 (PID 28071, ~120GB) + Llama4-Scout-W4A16 (PID 28924, ~50GB)

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **transfer_eval.py fix not deploying (flagged 4 times)**:
     - Worker 1 (worker_code_fix_transfer_eval_and_rerun): Recomputed CSV data using decision_function but didn't modify the source code
     - Worker 2 (worker_ops_deploy_transfer_eval_fix): Reported done but file unchanged (Modify time same, no decision_function found)
     - Worker 3 (worker_code_extract_activations_large): Wrote fixed version to `code/transfer_eval.py` but didn't copy to main location
     - Worker 4 (worker_ops_deploy_blocker1_blocker2): Finally deployed! Modify time updated, decision_function confirmed at line 73
     - My approach: Each time I independently verified via stat timestamps and grep, posted CRITICAL severity findings

   - **FP16 model downloads exceeding disk capacity**:
     - Initial downloads: Qwen3-235B FP16 (~470GB) + Llama4-Scout FP16 (~200GB) = 670GB > 350GB disk
     - I flagged CRITICAL with disk math
     - Fix: Downloads killed, replaced with quantized versions (GPTQ-Int4 ~120GB + W4A16 ~50GB)

   - **ROADMAP-reality inconsistencies**:
     - ROADMAP said "Llama4 FP16 serial" but actual was "both quantized parallel"
     - ROADMAP said Qwen3 ~65GB but actual GPTQ-Int4 is ~120GB
     - plan_002 says INT8 but actual download is W4A16
     - I flagged all three; first two were fixed in ROADMAP update, plan_002 description still pending

   - **ssh_read_only shell metacharacter limitations**: Commands with `|`, `<`, `>`, `;` blocked. Worked around by using separate grep calls without pipes, avoiding comparison operators in Python one-liners.

5. Problem Solving:
   - **zh@L31 mean-shift anomaly**: Independently analyzed transfer_eval.py code and data. Initial hypothesis was near-zero variance features in StandardScaler. Worker's more precise diagnosis: sigmoid saturation (logit mean=+39). Both explanations are related — extreme scaled values lead to sigmoid saturation. Fix: use decision_function for AUROC.
   - **CKA depth confound**: Identified that pooled Spearman correlation (overall rho=-0.40, p=0.007) is driven by between-language differences, not within-language depth effects. Per-language correlations mostly not significant (n=9 too small), ko shows reversed direction. Recommended same-layer cross-language correlation and partial correlation controlling for depth.
   - **Disk space management**: Tracked root partition from 98% → 4% via symlink migration, then prevented data disk overflow by flagging FP16 downloads early.
   - **P0 blocker tracking**: Maintained persistent tracking of 3 blockers across multiple worker notifications until resolution.

Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. 3	}
